1\leq b\leq n_{j}\end{subarray}}\omega_{i,j}(z_{i,a},z_{j,b})\right)}\right\}_{0},

where the rational function on the right hand side is expanded into a Laurent series assuming

\displaystyle|z_{i,a}|=1,

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{q}|<1,

\displaystyle\text{ and }

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{d}|=1.

3.4.
Feigin–Tsymbaliuk elements

One set of elements in
U_{\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{d}}(\ddot{\mathfrak{sl}}_{r})
to which we will apply the shuffle algebra machinery are those found by Feigin–Tsymbaliuk in
[
FT16
,
Tsy19
]
as
vacuum expectations of
L
-operators
.

3.4.1.
Dual bosons

For a fixed
n
, the matrix

\left(\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{-nm_{i,j}}[na_{i,j}]_{\mathfrak{q}}}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})n}\right)_{i,j}

is invertible.
Thus, by (
3.2
), we can define
dual bosons

\{b_{i,n}^{*}\}
satisfying

(3.7)

\left[b_{i,n}^{*},b_{j,n^{\prime}}\right]=\delta_{n,-n^{\prime}}\delta_{i,j}\left(\gamma^{n}-\gamma^{-n}\right).

Lemma 3.17
.

For
n>0
, we have

(3.8)

\displaystyle b_{i,n}^{*}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{n}(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})n}{(1-q^{nr})(1-t^{nr})[n]_{\mathfrak{q}}}\sum_{j,k=0}^{r-1}q^{nj}t^{nk}b_{i+j-k,n},\text{ and }

\displaystyle b_{i,-n}^{*}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{n}(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})n}{(1-q^{nr})(1-t^{nr})[n]_{\mathfrak{q}}}\sum_{j,k=0}^{r-1}q^{nj}t^{nk}b_{i-j+k,-n}

Proof.

This is by direct calculation, recalling that
q=\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{d}
and
t=\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{d}^{-1}
.
∎

Corollary 3.18
.

In terms of symmetric functions, the dual bosons act as follows: for
n>0
,

\displaystyle\rho_{\vec{\mathfrak{c}}}(b^{*}_{i,n})

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})p_{n}^{\perp}[X^{(i)}],\text{ and }

\displaystyle\rho_{\vec{\mathfrak{c}}}(b^{*}_{i,-n})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{q}^{n}(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})p_{n}\left[\frac{X^{(i)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(1-t\sigma)}\right].

Proof.

The first equation comes from (
3.3
) and (
3.7
).
Equation (
3.3
) also yields the second equation.
∎

3.4.2.
The elements
F_{p,\pm 1}

For
p\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}
, let

\displaystyle F_{p,1}\coloneq\frac{z_{p,1}}{z_{0,1}}\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}z_{i,1}

and

\displaystyle F_{p,-1}\coloneq\frac{z_{0,1}}{z_{p,1}}\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}z_{i,1}.

We note that both elements are in
\mathcal{S}_{\delta}
where
\delta=(1,\ldots,1)
is the diagonal vector.

Lemma 3.19

(
[
Tsy19
,
Wen19
]
)
.

We have

(3.9)

\displaystyle-(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})^{r-1}\Psi_{+}(F_{p,-1})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})}\varpi(b_{p,-1}^{*}),\text{ and }

(3.10)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{-r}\mathfrak{q}(\mathfrak{q}^{2}-1)^{r-1}\Psi_{-}\left(F_{p,1}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})}\varpi(b_{p,1}^{*}).

Proof.

By
[
Wen19
, B.2]
2
2

2

Note that our
b_{i,n}^{*}
is equal to
-b_{i,n}^{\perp}
from
loc. cit.
, we have

(-1)^{r-1}\mathfrak{d}^{-r}(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})^{r-1}\Psi_{+}(F_{p,1})=\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})}\varpi^{-1}(b_{p,1}^{*}).

We then obtain (
3.9
) by applying Proposition
3.15
.
Likewise, we obtain

(-1)^{r-1}\mathfrak{q}(\mathfrak{q}^{2}-1)^{r-1}\Psi_{-}\left(F_{p,-1}\right)=\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})}\varpi^{-1}(b_{p,-1}^{*})

by combining Lemma 4.11 and Theorem 4.12 from
[
Wen19
]
.
Equation (
3.10
) then follows by applying Proposition
3.15
.
∎

3.4.3.
The elements
\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}

For
i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}
, let

\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}\coloneq p_{1}\left[\frac{X^{(i)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(1-t\sigma)}\right].

These elements will play a key role later on.
Note that by Corollary
3.18
,

\displaystyle\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}=\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{-1}}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})}\rho_{\vec{\mathfrak{c}}}(b^{*}_{i,-1})

and

\displaystyle p_{1}^{\perp}[X^{(i)}]=\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})}\rho_{\vec{\mathfrak{c}}}(b_{i,1}^{*}).

Thus, they are related to the formulas in Lemma
3.19
via the Miki automorphism
\varpi
.

3.4.4.
The elements
F_{0,n}

Notice that
F_{0,1}=F_{0,-1}
.
To access nabla operators, we will also make use of higher degree analogues of
F_{0,1}
.
Namely, consider the following element of
\mathcal{S}_{n\delta}
:

F_{0,n}\coloneq\prod_{i=0}^{r}\prod_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}.

Setting
F_{0,0}=1
,

Lemma 3.20
.

We have

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{\frac{rn(n-1)}{2}+n}(\mathfrak{q}^{2}-1)^{nr}}{\mathfrak{q}^{n}\mathfrak{d}^{\frac{rn(n+1)}{2}}}\Psi_{-}(F_{0,n})z^{n}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\sum_{k>0}\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{-2k}-1}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})k}\varpi(b_{0,k}^{*})z^{k}\right).

Proof.

The following was proved in
[
Tsy19
]
(cf. Lemma 4.11 and Corollary 4.13 of
[
Wen19
]
, noting that
b_{0,n}^{*}=-b_{0,n}^{\perp}
):

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{\frac{rn(n+1)}{2}+n}(\mathfrak{q}^{2}-1)^{nr}}{\mathfrak{q}^{n}\mathfrak{d}^{\frac{rn(n-1)}{2}}}\Psi_{-}(F_{0,n})z^{n}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\sum_{k>0}\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{-2k}-1}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})k}\varpi^{-1}(b_{0,-k}^{*})z^{k}\right).

We obtain the second equality by applying Proposition
3.15
.
∎

3.4.5.
Eigenvalues

The bosonic expressions in Lemma
3.19
are in fact quite natural when one considers their action in the Fock representation.

Lemma 3.21
.

On the Fock representation
\mathcal{F}
, we have

(3.11)

\displaystyle\left\langle\lambda\left|\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})v}\tau_{v}(b_{p,1}^{*})\right|\lambda\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{-D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(p)},\text{ and }

\displaystyle\left\langle\lambda\left|\frac{v}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})}\tau_{v}(b_{p,-1}^{*})\right|\lambda\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{-D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(p)}_{*}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\lambda\left|\exp\left(\sum_{k>0}\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{-2k}-1}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})k}\tau_{v}(b_{0,k}^{*})\frac{z^{k}}{v^{k}}\right)\right|\lambda\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\Omega\left[-\left(\frac{(q^{-1}t^{-1}-1)D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(0)}z\right].

Proof.

We will only prove (
3.11
).
Lemma
3.6
gives us

\displaystyle\langle\lambda|\tau_{v}(b_{i,1})|\lambda\rangle

\displaystyle=-v\mathfrak{q}^{-1}D_{\lambda}^{(i)}.

Applying (
3.8
), consider

\displaystyle\left\langle\lambda\left|\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})v}\tau_{v}(b^{*}_{p,1})\right|\lambda\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-q^{r})(1-t^{r})}\sum_{j,k=0}^{r-1}q^{j}t^{k}\left(-D_{\lambda}^{(p+j-k)}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{-D_{\lambda}}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(p)}.\qed

Corollary 3.22
.

In
\mathcal{W}
, we have the following equalities:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})v}(\rho_{\vec{\mathfrak{c}}}^{-}\circ\varpi)(b_{p,1}^{*})(H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{-D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(p)}H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)},

\displaystyle\frac{v}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})}(\rho_{\vec{\mathfrak{c}}}^{+}\circ\varpi)(b_{p,-1}^{*})(H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{-D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(p)}_{*}H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)},

\displaystyle\exp\left(\sum_{k>0}\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{-2k}-1}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})k}(\rho_{\mathfrak{c}}^{-}\circ\varpi)(b_{0,-k}^{*})z^{n}\right)(H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)})

\displaystyle=\Omega\left[-\left(\frac{(q^{-1}t^{-1}-1)D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(0)}z\right]H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}.

Here,
v
and
\vec{\mathfrak{c}}
are related as in Theorem
3.7
.

3.4.6.
The series
\mathbb{D}
and
\mathbb{D}^{*}

Define

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}

\displaystyle\coloneq\frac{(1-qt)^{r-1}}{\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}(1-qz_{i+1}/z_{i})(1-tz_{i}/z_{i+1})}\Omega\left[-\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}z_{i}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\quad\times\mathcal{T}\left[(1-q\sigma)(1-t\sigma^{-1})\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}z_{i}^{-1}\right]\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}z_{i}^{-H_{i,0}},\text{ and }

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}^{*}

\displaystyle\coloneq\frac{(1-q^{-1}t^{-1})^{r-1}}{\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}(1-t^{-1}z_{i+1}/z_{i})(1-q^{-1}z_{i}/z_{i+1})}\Omega\left[\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}z_{i}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\quad\times\mathcal{T}\left[-(1-q^{-1}\sigma^{-1})(1-t^{-1}\sigma)\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}z_{i}^{-1}\right]\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}z_{i}^{H_{i,0}}.

For
\mathbb{D}
, we expand the rational functions as Laurent series assuming

\displaystyle|z_{i}|=1

\displaystyle\text{ and }

\displaystyle|q|,|t|<1;

whereas for
\mathbb{D}^{*}
, we expand assuming

\displaystyle|z_{i}|=1

\displaystyle\text{ and }

\displaystyle|q^{-1}|,|t^{-1}|<1.

Upon acting on
f\otimes e^{\alpha}\in\mathcal{W}
, we obtain Laurent series in the variables
z_{0},\cdots,z_{r-1}
.
Given a vector
\vec{k}=(k_{0},\ldots,k_{r-1})\in(\mathbb{Z})^{r}
, we define the operators
\mathbb{D}_{\vec{k}}
and
\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\vec{k}}
by setting

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{\vec{k}}(f\otimes e^{\alpha})

\displaystyle\coloneq\left\{z_{0}^{k_{0}}\cdots z_{r-1}^{k_{r-1}}\mathbb{D}(f\otimes e^{\alpha})\right\}_{0}\text{ and }

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\vec{k}}(f\otimes e^{\alpha})

\displaystyle\coloneq\left\{z_{0}^{k_{0}}\cdots z_{r-1}^{k_{r-1}}\mathbb{D}^{*}(f\otimes e^{\alpha})\right\}_{0}.

Let
\epsilon_{0},\ldots,\epsilon_{r-1}
be the coordinate vectors.

Lemma 3.23
.

For
p\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})v}(\rho_{\vec{\mathfrak{c}}}^{-}\circ\varpi)(b_{p,1}^{*})=\mathbb{D}_{\epsilon_{p}-\epsilon_{0}}

and

\displaystyle\frac{v}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})}(\rho_{\vec{\mathfrak{c}}}^{+}\circ\varpi)(b_{p,-1}^{*})=\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\epsilon_{0}-\epsilon_{p}}.

Proof.

We will only prove the first equality.
Combining Lemma
3.19
and Proposition
3.16
, we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})v}

\displaystyle(\rho_{\vec{\mathfrak{c}}}^{-}\circ\varpi)(b_{p,1}^{*})(f\otimes e^{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\left\{\frac{\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{-r}\mathfrak{q}(\mathfrak{q}^{2}-1)^{(r-1)}\frac{z_{p,1}}{z_{0,1}}\left(\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}z_{i,1}\right)\overset{\curvearrowleft}{\prod_{i=0}^{r-1}}F_{i}(z_{i,1})(f\otimes e^{\alpha})}{\displaystyle(-1)^{\frac{(r-2)(r-3)}{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{q}\mathfrak{d}^{-\frac{r}{2}}}{\mathfrak{c}_{0}\cdots\mathfrak{c}_{r-1}}\prod_{0\leq i<j\leq r-1}\omega_{i,j}(z_{i,1},z_{j,1})}\right\}_{0}

(3.12)

\displaystyle=\left\{\frac{\displaystyle(1-qt)^{(r-1)}\frac{z_{p,1}z_{r-1,1}}{z_{0,1}^{2}}\overset{\curvearrowleft}{\prod_{i=0}^{r-1}}F_{i}(z_{i,1})(f\otimes e^{\alpha})}{\displaystyle(-1)^{\frac{(r-2)(r-3)}{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{-\frac{r}{2}+1}}{\mathfrak{c}_{0}\cdots\mathfrak{c}_{r-1}}\left(1-tz_{r-1,1}/z_{0}\right)\prod_{i=0}^{r-2}\left(1-qz_{i+1}/z_{i}\right)}\right\}_{0},

and

\displaystyle\overset{\curvearrowleft}{\prod_{i=0}^{r-1}}F_{i}(z_{i,1})

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{\frac{(r-2)(r-3)}{2}}}{\mathfrak{d}^{\frac{r}{2}-1}\mathfrak{c}_{0}\cdots\mathfrak{c}_{r-1}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{z_{0,1}/z_{r-1,1}}{\displaystyle\left(1-qz_{0,1}/z_{r-1}\right)\prod_{i=0}^{r-2}\left(1-tz_{i,1}/z_{i+1}\right)}\Omega\left[-\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}z_{i,1}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\mathcal{T}\left[(1-q\sigma)(1-t\sigma^{-1})\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}z_{i,1}^{-1}\right]\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}z_{i,1}^{-H_{i,0}}.

Inserting this into (
3.12
), we do indeed obtain
\mathbb{D}_{\epsilon_{p}-\epsilon_{0}}(f\otimes e^{\alpha})
.
∎

Corollary 3.24
.

The operators
\mathbb{D}_{\epsilon_{p}-\epsilon_{0}}
and
\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\epsilon_{0}-\epsilon_{p}}
act diagonally on
H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}
with eigenvalues

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{\epsilon_{p}-\epsilon_{0}}(H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{-D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(p)}H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\text{ and }

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{\epsilon_{0}-\epsilon_{p}}^{*}(H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{-D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(p)}_{*}H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}.

3.5.
Wreath Macdonald operators

We have already seen some operators that act diagonally on
H_{\lambda}
.
Here, we make a deeper study of eigenoperators.

3.5.1.
Delta operators

We wish to define a ring homomorphism
\Delta:\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}\rightarrow\mathrm{End}_{\mathbb{F}}(\mathcal{W})
.
First, we define the embedding
\epsilon^{+}:\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}\rightarrow\varpi(\mathcal{H})
by setting

p_{k}[X^{(i)}]\mapsto\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{k}k}{v^{k}[k]_{\mathfrak{q}}}\varpi(b_{i,k}).

We then define
\Delta\coloneq\rho_{\vec{\mathfrak{c}}}^{-}\circ\epsilon^{+}
and denote its inputs using square brackets.
By Theorem
3.8
, the image of
\Delta
acts diagonally on
\{H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\}
.
The following is a corollary of Lemma
3.6
:

Corollary 3.25
.

For any
f\in\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}
, we have

\Delta[f](H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)})=f[-D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}]H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}.

Using (
3.8
), Corollary
3.24
implies

Proposition 3.26
.

We have

\Delta\left[p_{1}\left[\frac{X^{(i)}}{(1-q\sigma)(1-t\sigma^{-1})}\right]\right]=\Delta\left[\tilde{p}_{1}^{(-i)}[\iota X^{\bullet}]\right]=\mathbb{D}_{\epsilon_{i}-\epsilon_{0}}.

3.5.2.
Column-type operators

We now consider a modified elementary symmetric function

\hat{e}_{n}^{(p)}=e_{n}\left[\frac{X^{(p)}}{1-t^{-1}\sigma^{-1}}\right]=t^{n}e_{n}\left[\frac{-X^{(p+1)}}{1-t\sigma}\right],

for which we have

\hat{e}_{n}^{(-p-1)}[-\iota X^{\bullet}]=t^{n}e_{n}\left[-\iota\left(\frac{-X^{((-p-1)+1)}}{1-t\sigma}\right)\right]=t^{n}e_{n}\left[\frac{X^{(p)}}{1-t\sigma^{-1}}\right].

Theorem 3.27

(
[
OSW22
]
)
.

We have

(3.13)

\displaystyle\Delta\left[\hat{e}_{n}^{(-p-1)}[-\iota X^{\bullet}]\right](f\otimes e^{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\frac{t^{n}(1-qt)^{nr}}{\prod_{k=1}^{n}(1-q^{k}t^{k})}\left\{\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq n}\left[\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\frac{\left(1-z_{p,a}/z_{p,b}\right)\left(1-qtz_{p,a}/z_{p,b}\right)}{\left(1-t^{-1}z_{p+1,a}/z_{p,b}\right)\left(1-tz_{p-1,a}/z_{p,b}\right)}

\displaystyle\times\left.\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{p\}}\frac{\left(1-z_{i,a}/z_{i,b}\right)\left(1-qtz_{i,a}/z_{i,b}\right)}{\left(1-qz_{i+1,a}/z_{i,b}\right)\left(1-tz_{i-1,a}/z_{i,b}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle\times\prod_{a=1}^{n}\left[\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{p\}}\left(\frac{1}{1-tz_{i,a}/z_{i+1,a}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-qz_{i+1,a}/z_{i,a}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{z_{p,a}}{z_{0,a}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-tz_{p,a}/z_{p+1,a}}\right)\left]\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\Omega\left[-\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}\left(\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\left.\quad\times\TT\left[(1-q\sigma)(1-t\sigma^{-1})\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}\left(\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}^{-1}\right)\right]f\otimes\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}\prod_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}^{-H_{i,0}}e^{\alpha}\right\}_{0}.

In particular,

\Delta\left[\hat{e}_{n}^{(-p-1)}[-\iota X^{\bullet}]\right](H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)})=t^{n}e_{n}\left[\left(\frac{-D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{1-t}\right)^{(p)}\right]H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}.

Proof.

The expression (
3.13
) is gotten from
[
OSW22
]
.
Namely, we wish to apply
\rho_{\vec{\mathfrak{c}}}^{-}
to the coefficient of
z^{n}
in

\varpi\exp\left[-\sum_{k>0}\left(\frac{\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}t^{k(i+1)}b_{p-i,k}}{(1-t^{kr})}\right)\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{k}}{v^{k}[k]_{\mathfrak{q}}}(-z)^{k}\right].

This is exactly the expression on the right-hand side of the second equation of
[
OSW22
, Lemma 4.4]
3
3

3

Note that our
\varpi
is denoted by
\varsigma
and our
b_{i,k}
is denoted by
h_{i,k}
.
.
The aforementioned lemma computes
\Psi_{-}^{-1}
of this coefficient.
We can then use Proposition
3.16
to compute the action of this shuffle element on
\mathcal{W}
.
The end result is given by the second equation in
[
OSW22
, Lemma 5.2]
.
Note that
loc. cit.
considers the finite-variable situation and their vertex representation differs from ours; thus, the
\Omega
- and
\TT
-terms need to be adjusted.
For the eigenvalue, we use Corollary
3.25
:

\displaystyle\frac{X^{(p)}}{1-t\sigma^{-1}}\bigg|_{X^{(i)}\mapsto-D_{\lambda}^{(i)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}t^{i}X^{(p-i)}}{1-t^{r}}\bigg|_{X^{(i)}\mapsto-D_{\lambda}^{(i)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{-\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}t^{i}D_{\lambda}^{(p-i)}}{1-t^{r}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{-D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{1-t}\right)^{(p)}.\qed

3.5.3.
Row-type operators

We make a similar study of a modified complete homogeneous symmetric function

\hat{h}_{n}^{(p)}=h_{n}\left[\frac{X^{(p)}}{1-q\sigma^{-1}}\right].

Notice that we have

\hat{h}_{n}^{(-p)}[-\iota X^{\bullet}]=h_{n}\left[\frac{-X^{(p)}}{1-q\sigma}\right].

Theorem 3.28

(
[
OSW22
]
)
.

We have

\displaystyle\Delta\left[\hat{h}_{n}^{(-p)}[-\iota X^{\bullet}]\right](f\otimes e^{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{n}\left(1-qt\right)^{nr}}{\prod_{k=1}^{n}\left(1-q^{k}t^{k}\right)}\left\{\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq n}\left[\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\frac{\left(1-z_{p+1,a}/z_{p+1,b}\right)\left(1-qtz_{p+1,a}/z_{p+1,b}\right)}{\left(1-q^{-1}z_{p,a}/z_{p+1,b}\right)\left(1-qz_{p-2,a}/z_{p+1,b}\right)}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left.\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{p+1\}}\frac{\left(1-z_{i,a}/z_{i,b}\right)\left(1-qtz_{i,a}/z_{i,b}\right)}{\left(1-qz_{i+1,a}/z_{i,b}\right)\left(1-tz_{i-1,a}/z_{i,b}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{a=1}^{n}\left[\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{p+1\}}\left(\frac{1}{1-qz_{i,a}/z_{i-1,a}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-tz_{i-1,a}/z_{i,a}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\frac{z_{p+1,a}}{z_{0,a}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-qz_{p+1,a}/z_{p,a}}\right)\left]\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\Omega\left[-\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}\left(\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\left.\times\TT\left[(1-q\sigma)(1-t\sigma^{-1})\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}\left(\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}^{-1}\right)\right]f\otimes\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}\prod_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}^{-H_{i,0}}e^{\alpha}\right\}_{0}

and

\displaystyle\Delta\left[\hat{h}_{n}^{(-p)}[-\iota X^{\bullet}]\right](H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)})

\displaystyle=h_{n}\left[\left(\frac{D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{1-q}\right)^{(p)}\right]H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}.

Here, we expand the character on the right hand side assuming
|q|<1
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Theorem
3.27
.
Namely, the end of
[
OSW22
, A.2]
gives shuffle elements corresponding to the coefficients of

\varpi\exp\left[-\sum_{k>0}\left(\frac{\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}q^{ki}b_{p+i,k}}{1-q^{kr}}\right)\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{k}}{v^{k}[k]_{\mathfrak{q}}}z^{k}\right].

We apply Proposition
3.16
, the result of which is similar to
[
OSW22
, A.4]
.
∎

3.5.4.
Operators from
F_{0,n}

Let

e_{F,n}^{(0)}\coloneq e_{n}\left[\frac{(q^{-1}t^{-1}-1)X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma)(t\sigma^{-1}-1)}\right].

We will compute one more family of Delta operators.

Lemma 3.29
.

We have

(3.14)

\displaystyle\Delta\left[e_{F,n}^{(0)}\right]

\displaystyle(f\otimes e^{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-qt)^{nr}}{\prod_{k=1}^{n}(1-q^{k}t^{k})}\left\{\prod_{1\leq a\not=b\leq n}\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\left(1-z_{i,a}/z_{i,b}\right)\left(1-qtz_{i,a}/z_{i,b}\right)}{\left(1-qz_{i+1,a}/z_{i,b}\right)\left(1-tz_{i-1,a}/z_{i,b}\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\times\prod_{a=1}^{n}\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}\left(\frac{1}{1-tz_{i,a}/z_{i+1,a}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-qz_{i+1,a}/z_{i,a}}\right)\Omega\left[-\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}\left(\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\left.\quad\times\TT\left[(1-q\sigma)(1-t\sigma^{-1})\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}\left(\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}^{-1}\right)\right]f\otimes\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}\prod_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}^{-H_{i,0}}e^{\alpha}\right\}_{0}

and

\Delta\left[e_{F,n}^{(0)}\right](H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)})=e_{n}\left[(q^{-1}t^{-1}-1)\left(B_{\lambda}^{\bullet}-\frac{1}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(0)}\right]H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}.

Proof.

First observe that by (
3.8
),

\displaystyle\epsilon^{+}\left(p_{k}\left[\frac{(q^{-1}t^{-1}-1)X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma)(t\sigma^{-1}-1)}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{(\mathfrak{q}^{-2k}-1)\mathfrak{q}^{k}k}{v^{k}[k]_{\mathfrak{q}}}\frac{\sum_{j,k=0}^{r-1}q^{nj}t^{nk}b_{j-k,k}}{(1-q^{nr})(1-t^{nr})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{(\mathfrak{q}^{-2k}-1)}{(\mathfrak{q}-\mathfrak{q}^{-1})v^{k}}b_{0,k}^{*}.

Thus, by Lemma
3.20
,

\Delta\left[e_{F,n}^{(0)}\right]=\frac{(-1)^{\frac{rn(n-1)}{2}+n}(\mathfrak{q}^{2}-1)^{nr}}{\mathfrak{q}^{n}\mathfrak{d}^{\frac{rn(n+1)}{2}}v^{n}}\left(\rho_{\vec{\mathfrak{c}}}^{-}\circ\Psi_{-}\right)(F_{0,n}).

We obtain (
3.14
) by applying Proposition
3.16
and
[
OSW22
, (3.12)]
.
For the eigenvalue, note that

\frac{-D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(t-1)}=B_{\lambda}^{\bullet}-\frac{1}{(1-q)(1-t)}.\qed

3.5.5.
Nabla revisited

Recall the nabla operator
\nabla_{\alpha}
, which depends on an
r
-core
\alpha.

We extend its definiton to
\Lambda_{q,t}\otimes\mathbb{C}[Q]
by setting

\nabla\left(f\otimes e^{\alpha}\right)=\nabla_{\alpha}(f)\otimes e^{\alpha}.

Lemma 3.30
.

For a fixed degree
N
, there exists an expression
\mathcal{E}_{N}
in terms of
\{e_{F,n}^{(0)}\}
such that for
f\in\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}
of degree
N
,

\nabla\left(f\otimes e^{\alpha}\right)=\frac{(-1)^{N}\Delta\left[\mathcal{E}_{N}\right](f\otimes e^{\alpha})}{\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\square\in\mathrm{core}(\lambda)\\
\bar{c}_{\square}=0\end{subarray}}\chi_{\square}}.

We emphasize that
\mathcal{E}_{N}
is independent of
\alpha
.

Proof.

We will prove the lemma by considering the eigenvalues on
H_{\lambda}
with
|\mathrm{quot}(\lambda)|=N
.
Notice that

\frac{q^{-1}t^{-1}-1}{(1-q)(1-t)}=\frac{q^{-1}t^{-1}}{1-t}+\frac{t^{-1}}{1-q},

and since
q^{-1}t^{-1}
has color
0
,

\displaystyle(q^{-1}t^{-1}-1)\left(\frac{1}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(0)}=\left(\frac{q^{-1}t^{-1}-1}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(0)}.

Thus,

(q^{-1}t^{-1}-1)\left(B_{\lambda}^{\bullet}-\frac{1}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(0)}=(q^{-1}t^{-1}-1)B_{\lambda}^{(0)}-\frac{q^{-1}t^{-1}}{1-t^{r}}-\frac{q^{r-1}t^{-1}}{1-q^{r}}.

We can then write
e_{N}[B_{\lambda}^{(0)}]
in terms of

\displaystyle e_{n}\left[(q^{-1}t^{-1}-1)B_{\lambda}^{(0)}-\frac{q^{-1}t^{-1}}{1-t^{r}}-\frac{q^{r-1}t^{-1}}{1-q^{r}}\right],

\displaystyle e_{n}\left[-\frac{q^{-1}t^{-1}}{1-t^{r}}\right],

\displaystyle\text{ and }

\displaystyle e_{n}\left[-\frac{q^{r-1}t^{-1}}{1-q^{r}}\right].

The latter two are rational functions in
\mathbb{C}(q,t)
, and thus we obtain
\mathcal{E}_{N}
.
∎

3.5.6.
Adjoints

Recall how in
3.2.6
, we extended the pairing
\langle-,-\rangle_{q,t}^{\prime}
to
\mathcal{W}
in a somewhat peculiar way.
Let
\Delta^{\dagger}
denote the composition of
\Delta
with the adjunction.
Thus, we have

(3.15)

\Delta^{\dagger}[f](H_{\lambda}^{\dagger}\otimes e^{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)})=f[-D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}]H_{\lambda}^{\dagger}\otimes e^{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}.

More generally, for an operator
M
, we denote its adjoint by
M^{\dagger}
.
To explicitly compute adjoint operators when the currents
\{F_{i}(z)\}
are involved, Lemmas
3.10
and
3.11
tell us to send the variables
z_{i,a}\mapsto z_{-i,a}^{-1}
.
When working with
n
currents of each color, we will in fact perform
z_{i,a}\mapsto z_{-i,n+1-a}^{-1}
.

Lemma 3.31
.

Taking adjoints gives us the following families of operators:

(1)

For
\vec{k}=(k_{0},\ldots,k_{r-1})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
, let
\iota\vec{k}=(k_{0},k_{r-1},\ldots,k_{1})
and
|\vec{k}|=\sum k_{i}
.
Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{\vec{k}}^{\dagger}=\mathbb{D}_{-\iota\vec{k}}

\displaystyle\text{ and }

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{D}_{\vec{k}}^{*}\right)^{\dagger}=(qt)^{-|\vec{k}|}\mathbb{D}_{-\iota\vec{k}}^{*}.

In particular, from
\mathbb{D}_{\epsilon_{p}-\epsilon_{0}}
and
\mathbb{D}_{\epsilon_{0}-\epsilon_{p}}^{*}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{\epsilon_{0}-\epsilon_{p}}(H_{\lambda}^{\dagger}\otimes e^{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{-D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(-p)}H_{\lambda}^{\dagger}\otimes e^{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\text{ and }

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{\epsilon_{p}-\epsilon_{0}}^{*}(H_{\lambda}^{\dagger}\otimes e^{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{-D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(-p)}_{*}H_{\lambda}^{\dagger}\otimes e^{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}.

(2)

The column-type operators become

(3.16)

\displaystyle\Delta^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\left[\hat{e}_{n}^{(p)}[-\iota X^{\bullet}]\right](f\otimes e^{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\frac{t^{n}(1-qt)^{nr}}{\prod_{k=1}^{n}(1-q^{k}t^{k})}\left\{\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq n}\left[\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\frac{\left(1-z_{p,a}/z_{p,b}\right)\left(1-qtz_{p,a}/z_{p,b}\right)}{\left(1-t^{-1}z_{p+1,a}/z_{p,b}\right)\left(1-tz_{p-1,a}/z_{p,b}\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{p\}}\frac{\left(1-z_{i,a}/z_{i,b}\right)\left(1-qtz_{i,a}/z_{i,b}\right)}{\left(1-qz_{i+1,a}/z_{i,b}\right)\left(1-tz_{i-1,a}/z_{i,b}\right)}\left]\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.

\displaystyle\times\prod_{a=1}^{n}\left[\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{p\}}\left(\frac{1}{1-tz_{i,a}/z_{i+1,a}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-qz_{i+1,a}/z_{i,a}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{z_{0,a}}{z_{p+1,a}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-tz_{p,a}/z_{p+1,a}}\right)\left]\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\Omega\left[-\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}\left(\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\left.\quad\times\TT\left[(1-q\sigma)(1-t\sigma^{-1})\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}\left(\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}^{-1}\right)\right]f\otimes\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}\prod_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}^{-H_{i,0}}e^{\alpha}\right\}_{0}.

(3)

The row-type operators become

(3.17)

\displaystyle\Delta^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\left[\hat{h}_{n}^{(p)}[-\iota X^{\bullet}]\right](f\otimes e^{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{n}\left(1-qt\right)^{nr}}{\prod_{k=1}^{n}\left(1-q^{k}t^{k}\right)}\left\{\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq n}\left[\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\frac{\left(1-z_{p,a}/z_{p,b}\right)\left(1-qtz_{p,a}/z_{p,b}\right)}{\left(1-q^{-1}z_{p-1,a}/z_{p,b}\right)\left(1-qz_{p+1,a}/z_{p,b}\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{p\}}\frac{\left(1-z_{i,a}/z_{i,b}\right)\left(1-qtz_{i,a}/z_{i,b}\right)}{\left(1-qz_{i+1,a}/z_{i,b}\right)\left(1-tz_{i-1,a}/z_{i,b}\right)}\left]\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.

\displaystyle\times\prod_{a=1}^{n}\left[\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{p\}}\left(\frac{1}{1-qz_{i,a}/z_{i-1,a}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-tz_{i-1,a}/z_{i,a}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{z_{0,a}}{z_{p,a}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{1-qz_{p,a}/z_{p-1,a}}\right)\left]\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\Omega\left[-\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}\left(\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\left.\times\TT\left[(1-q\sigma)(1-t\sigma^{-1})\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}\left(\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}^{-1}\right)\right]f\otimes\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}\prod_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}^{-H_{i,0}}e^{\alpha}\right\}_{0}.

(4)

\Delta\left[e_{F,n}^{(0)}\right]
is self-adjoint.

Corollary 3.32
.

The adjoint of
\nabla_{\alpha}
is
\nabla_{w_{0}\alpha}
.
In other words,
\nabla
is self-adjoint.

Proof.

Because
\Delta\left[e_{F,n}^{(0)}\right]
is self-adjoint, the expression
\Delta[\mathcal{E}_{N}]
from Lemma
3.30
is self-adjoint as well.
Proposition
2.4
then tells us

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\square\in\alpha\\
\bar{c}_{\square}=0\end{subarray}}\chi_{\square}=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\square\in w_{0}\alpha\\
\bar{c}_{\square}=0\end{subarray}}\chi_{\square}.\qed

4.
Tesler identity

4.1.
Setup

Here, we gather some ingredients necessary for our proofs.

4.1.1.
Constant term lemma

We will make use of the following lemma for computing constant terms:

Lemma 4.1
.

Suppose we are taking the constant term of an expression

\frac{zF(z)}{(z-P_{1})\cdots(z-P_{k})}

with respect to the variable
z
, where

•

F(z)
is a series in nonnegative powers of
z
;

•

each pole is simple and nonzero; and

•

each pole is expanded as

\frac{1}{z-P_{i}}=z^{-1}\sum_{n\geq 0}\frac{P_{i}^{n}}{z^{n}}.

Then the constant term is a sum over evaluations:

\left\{\frac{zF(z)}{(z-P_{1})\cdots(z-P_{k})}\right\}_{0}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{F(P_{i})}{\displaystyle\prod_{j\not=i}(P_{i}-P_{j})}.

Proof.

Applying the partial fraction decomposition to
\prod_{i=1}^{k}(z-P_{i})^{-1}
, we have

\frac{z}{(z-P_{1})\cdots(z-P_{k})}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{1}{\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{P_{i}}{z}\right)\prod_{j\not=i}(P_{i}-P_{j})}.

Furthermore, if
F(z)=\sum_{k\geq 0}F_{k}z^{k}
, then

\left\{\frac{\sum_{k}z^{k}F_{k}}{1-P_{i}/z}\right\}_{0}={\sum_{k\geq 0}P_{i}^{k}F_{k}}=F(P_{i}).\qed

4.1.2.
Down arrow

To pass between
\mathbb{D}
and
\mathbb{D}^{*}
, we will make use of an analogue of the
\downarrow
map from
[
GHT99
]
.
This was first presented in
[
OS23
]
on
\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}
, and we make the following extension to
\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}\otimes\mathbb{C}[Q]
:

\downarrow\left(f\otimes e^{\alpha}\right)\coloneq f\left[-\iota X^{\bullet};q^{-1},t^{-1}\right]\otimes e^{w_{0}\alpha}.

Thus,
\downarrow
is only
\mathbb{C}
-linear as it inverts
(q,t)
.
Our choice of extension to the
\mathbb{C}[Q]
factor is motivated by the following, which is straightforward:

Proposition 4.2
.

The involution
\downarrow
satisfies:

(1)

\downarrow\left(H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\right)=H_{\lambda}^{\dagger}\otimes e^{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}
,

(2)

\downarrow\nabla_{\alpha}\downarrow=\nabla^{-1}_{w_{0}\alpha}
,

(3)

For any
\vec{k}=(k_{0},k_{1},\ldots,k_{r-1})\in\mathbb{Z}^{r}
, let
\iota\vec{k}=(k_{0},k_{r-1},\ldots,k_{1})
.
Then
\downarrow\mathbb{D}_{\vec{k}}\downarrow=\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\iota\vec{k}}
.

4.1.3.
Containment lemma

We note that the proof of the following result uses the quantum toroidal algebra:

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
f\in\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}
be homogeneous of degree
n
.
Then

(4.1)

\displaystyle fH_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\\
\mathrm{core}(\mu)=\mathrm{core}(\lambda)\end{subarray}}c_{\mu\lambda}H_{\mu}~\text{ and }

(4.2)

\displaystyle fH_{\lambda}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\\
\mathrm{core}(\mu)=\mathrm{core}(\lambda)\end{subarray}}c_{\mu\lambda}^{\dagger}H^{\dagger}_{\mu},

where the
\mu
that appear in either sum are such that
\mu\supset\lambda
and
\mu\backslash\lambda
contains exactly of
n
boxes of each color.
Similarly,

(4.3)

\displaystyle f^{\perp}H_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\\
\mathrm{core}(\mu)=\mathrm{core}(\lambda)\end{subarray}}d_{\mu\lambda}H_{\mu}~\text{ and }

\displaystyle f^{\perp}H_{\lambda}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\\
\mathrm{core}(\mu)=\mathrm{core}(\lambda)\end{subarray}}d_{\mu\lambda}^{\dagger}H^{\dagger}_{\mu},

where the
\mu
that appear in either sum are such that
\mu\subset\lambda
and
\lambda\backslash\mu
contains exactly
n
boxes of each color.

Proof.

Let us embed
\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}
into
U_{\mathfrak{q},\mathfrak{d}}(\ddot{\mathfrak{sl}}_{r})
compatibly with (
3.3
):

p_{n}[X^{(i)}]=\frac{n}{[n]_{\mathfrak{q}}}b_{i,-n}

Recall the elements
\{\hat{e}_{n}^{(p)}\}
from
2.3.2
.
Combining Proposition 4.23 and Theorem 4.31 from
[
Wen19
]
, we have that in the Fock representation
\mathcal{F}
,

\left\langle\mu\left|(\tau_{v}\circ\varpi^{-1})(\hat{e}_{n}^{(p)})\right|\lambda\right\rangle

is nonzero only if
\mu\supset\lambda
and
\mu\backslash\lambda
contains exactly
n
boxes of each color.
Applying Theorems
3.7
and
3.8
, we obtain (
4.1
) when
f=\hat{e}_{n}^{(p)}
.
Since
\{\hat{e}_{n}^{(p)}\}
generates
\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}
algebraically, we obtain (
4.1
) for general
f
.
By Proposition
4.2
(1), (
4.2
) follows from applying
\downarrow
to (
4.1
).
Finally, by Lemma
3.10
, we have

\left\langle g,f^{\perp}h\right\rangle_{q,t}^{\prime}=\left\langle f\left[\frac{\iota X^{\bullet}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right]g,h\right\rangle_{q,t}^{\prime}

Thus, (
4.3
) follows from (
4.1
) and (
4.2
).
∎

4.1.4.
Relations

Here, we record some commutation relations involving
\mathbb{D}
and
\mathbb{D}^{*}
.
These are completely analogous to those catalogued in
[
GHT99
]
.

Lemma 4.4
.

The following relations hold:

(4.4)

\displaystyle\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(i)}w}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(1-t\sigma)}\right]\mathbb{D}

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{w}{z_{i}}\right)\mathbb{D}\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(i)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(1-t\sigma)}w\right],

(4.5)

\displaystyle\TT\left[X^{(i)}w\right]\mathbb{D}

\displaystyle=\left(1-z_{i}w\right)\mathbb{D}\TT\left[X^{(i)}w\right],

(4.6)

\displaystyle\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(i)}w}{(1-q^{-1}\sigma)(t^{-1}\sigma^{-1}-1)}\right]\mathbb{D}^{*}

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{w}{z_{i}}\right)\mathbb{D}^{*}\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(i)}}{(1-q^{-1}\sigma)(t^{-1}\sigma^{-1}-1)}w\right],

(4.7)

\displaystyle\TT\left[-X^{(i)}w\right]\mathbb{D}^{*}

\displaystyle=\left(1-z_{i}w\right)\mathbb{D}^{*}\TT\left[-X^{(i)}w\right].

Proof.

These all follow from an application of Lemma
3.9
.
We will only go through the proof of (
4.4
).
The
\Omega
-term in the equation will only interact nontrivially with the
\mathcal{T}
-term of
\mathbb{D}
, which is

\TT\left[(1-q\sigma)(1-t\sigma^{-1})\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(j)}z_{j}^{-1}\right].

By Proposition
2.7
(2), we have

\left[(1-q\sigma)(1-t\sigma^{-1})\right]^{T}=(1-q\sigma^{-1})(1-t\sigma).

Thus, Lemma
3.9
gives

\displaystyle\Omega

\displaystyle\left[\frac{X^{(i)}w}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(1-t\sigma)}\right]\TT\left[(1-q\sigma)(1-t\sigma^{-1})\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(j)}z_{j}^{-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\Omega\left[-\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}\delta_{i,j}\frac{w}{z_{j}}\right]\TT\left[(1-q\sigma)(1-t\sigma^{-1})\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(j)}z_{j}^{-1}\right]\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(i)}w}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(1-t\sigma)}\right]

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{w}{z_{i}}\right)\TT\left[(1-q\sigma)(1-t\sigma^{-1})\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(j)}z_{j}^{-1}\right]\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(i)}w}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(1-t\sigma)}\right].\qed

Corollary 4.5
.

Recall the elements
\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}
from
3.4.3
.

(1)

For any vector
\vec{k}=(k_{0},\ldots,k_{r-1})\in\mathbb{Z}^{r}
, the following hold:

(4.8)

\displaystyle\left[\mathbb{D}_{\vec{k}},\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{D}_{\vec{k}-\epsilon_{i}},

(4.9)

\displaystyle\left[\mathbb{D}_{\vec{k}},p_{1}^{\perp}[X^{(i)}]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{D}_{\vec{k}+\epsilon_{i}},

(4.10)

\displaystyle\left[\mathbb{D}_{\vec{k}}^{*},\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}\right]

\displaystyle=-qt\mathbb{D}_{\vec{k}-\epsilon_{i}}^{*},

(4.11)

\displaystyle\left[\mathbb{D}_{\vec{k}}^{*},p_{1}^{\perp}[X^{(i)}]\right]

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{D}_{\vec{k}+\epsilon_{i}}^{*}.

(2)

We also have the following relations:

(4.12)

\displaystyle\nabla\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}\nabla^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{i}}

(4.13)

\displaystyle\nabla^{-1}p_{1}^{\perp}[X^{(i)}]\nabla

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{D}_{\epsilon_{i}},

(4.14)

\displaystyle\nabla^{-1}\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}\nabla

\displaystyle=-qt\mathbb{D}^{*}_{-\epsilon_{i}},

(4.15)

\displaystyle\nabla p_{1}^{\perp}[X^{(i)}]\nabla^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{D}_{\epsilon_{i}}^{*}.

Proof.

Part (1) follows from Lemma
4.4
.
For instance, for (
4.8
), we take the coefficient of
wz^{-k_{0}}\cdots z^{-k_{r-1}}
in (
4.4
).
For (
4.10
), we note that:

\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}=p_{1}\left[\frac{X^{(i)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(1-t\sigma)}\right]=-q^{-1}t^{-1}p_{1}\left[\frac{X^{(i)}}{(1-q^{-1}\sigma)(t^{-1}\sigma^{-1}-1)}\right]

Part (2) follows from combining part (1) with Lemma
4.3
and Corollary
3.24
.
We will only prove (
4.12
).
First note that by (
4.8
),

\left[\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}},\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}\right]=\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{i}}.

Consider now the action of the left-hand-side on
H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}
.
Lemma
4.3
says that

\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}H_{\lambda}=\sum_{\mu}c_{\lambda\mu}H_{\mu}

where the
\mu
are such that
\mu\supset\lambda
and
\mu\backslash\lambda
contains exactly one box of each color.
On the other hand, Corollary
3.24
tells us that

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}}\left(H_{\nu}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\nu)}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{-D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(0)}\left(H_{\nu}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\nu)}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\left(\frac{1}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(0)}-B_{\nu}^{(0)}\right)H_{\nu}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\nu)}.

Since only one color
0
box
\square_{\mu}
is in
\mu\backslash\lambda
, we have

\displaystyle\left[\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}},\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}\right]\left(H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu}c_{\lambda\mu}\left(-B_{\mu}^{(0)}+B_{\lambda}^{(0)}\right)\left(H_{\mu}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu}c_{\lambda\mu}\left(-\chi_{\square_{\mu}}\right)\left(H_{\mu}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\right)

\displaystyle=\nabla\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}\nabla^{-1}\left(H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\right).\qed

4.1.5.
Induction

We will need an analogue of
[
GHT99
, Theorem 2.1]
.
First, we prove the following:

Theorem 4.6
.

Every element
f\otimes e^{\alpha}\in\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}\otimes\mathbb{C}[Q]
may be written as

(4.16)

\displaystyle f\otimes e^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=P\left(\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{i}},\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}\right)(1\otimes e^{\alpha})

for some noncommutative polynomial
P
(with coefficients in
\mathbb{C}(q,t)
) in the operators
\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{0}},\ldots,\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{r-1}}
and
\tilde{p}_{1}^{(0)},\ldots,\tilde{p}_{1}^{(r-1)}
.
Likewise, we can write

(4.17)

f\otimes e^{\alpha}=P^{*}\left(\mathbb{D}^{*}_{-\epsilon_{i}},\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}\right)(1\otimes e^{\alpha})

for some noncommutative polynomial
P^{*}
in the operators
\mathbb{D}^{*}_{-\epsilon_{0}},\ldots,\mathbb{D}^{*}_{-\epsilon_{r-1}}
and
\tilde{p}_{1}^{(0)},\ldots,\tilde{p}_{1}^{(r-1)}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.2
, it suffices to prove (
4.16
).
Let

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{n}^{(p)}=h_{n}\left[\frac{X^{(p)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(1-t\sigma)}\right]

and

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{\vec{\lambda}}=h_{\vec{\lambda}}\left[\frac{X^{\bullet}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(1-t\sigma)}\right].

We first study the basis given by
\{\tilde{h}_{\vec{\lambda}}\otimes e^{\alpha}\}
.
Let us set

R_{a_{1},\dots,a_{k}}\coloneq\Omega\left[\sum_{j=1}^{k}w_{j}\frac{X^{(a_{j})}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(1-t\sigma)}\right](1\otimes e^{\alpha})=\sum_{m_{1},\dots,m_{k}\geq 0}\prod_{j=1}^{k}w_{j}^{m_{j}}\tilde{h}^{(a_{j})}_{m_{j}}\otimes e^{\alpha}.

Any complete homogeneous symmetric function
\tilde{h}_{\vec{\lambda}}
can be realized as a coefficient of some
R_{a_{1},\ldots,a_{k}}
by choosing (possibly repeating) suitable colors
a_{i}
.

First note that for given colors
a_{1},\dots,a_{k}
, by (
4.4
),

(4.18)

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{k}(1-w_{j}/z_{a_{j}})~

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}\Omega\left[\sum_{j=1}^{k}w_{j}\frac{X^{(a_{j})}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(1-t\sigma)}\right](1\otimes e^{\alpha})

(4.19)

\displaystyle=\Omega\left[\sum_{j=1}^{k}w_{j}\frac{X^{(a_{j})}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(1-t\sigma)}\right]\mathbb{D}(1\otimes e^{\alpha}).

We will compare the constant terms in the
z
-variables of both sides.
By Corollary
3.24
,
\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}}(1\otimes e^{\alpha})=C_{\alpha}(1\otimes e^{\alpha})
, where

C_{\alpha}=\left(\frac{-D_{\alpha}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(0)}=-B_{\alpha}^{(0)}+\left(\frac{1}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(0)}\not=0.

Thus, taking the constant term in the
z
-variables in (
4.19
) gives us

(4.20)

\displaystyle\left\{\Omega\left[\sum_{j=1}^{k}w_{j}\frac{X^{(a_{j})}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(1-t\sigma)}\right]\mathbb{D}(1\otimes e^{\alpha})\right\}_{0}=C_{\alpha}R_{a_{1},\ldots,a_{k}}.

On the other hand, since
\left\{(z_{b_{1}}\cdots z_{b_{l}})^{-1}\mathbb{D}\right\}_{0}=\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{b_{1}}-\cdots-\epsilon_{b_{l}}}
, the constant term of the
z
-variables in (
4.18
) can be written as:

\displaystyle\left\{\prod_{j=1}^{k}(1-w_{j}/z_{a_{j}})~\mathbb{D}\Omega\left[\sum_{j}w_{j}\frac{X^{(a_{j})}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(1-t\sigma)}\right](1\otimes e^{\alpha})\right\}_{0}

(4.21)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt=\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}}\left(R_{a_{1},\dots,a_{k}}\right)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varnothing\not=\{i_{1},\dots,i_{l}\}\\
\hskip 16.38895pt\subseteq\{1,\dots,k\}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{l}w_{i_{1}}\cdots w_{i_{l}}\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{a_{i_{1}}}-\cdots-\epsilon_{a_{i_{l}}}}R_{a_{1},\dots,a_{k}}.

We will instead write
\{b_{1},\dots,b_{l}\}\subseteq\{a_{1},\dots,a_{k}\}
to mean that
b_{j}=a_{i_{j}}
for
\{i_{1},\dots,i_{l}\}\subseteq\{1,\dots,k\}
.
Extracting the coefficients of
w_{1}^{m_{1}}\cdots w_{k}^{m_{k}}
from (
4.20
) and (
4.21
) and equating them, we get

\displaystyle C_{\alpha}\tilde{h}^{(a_{1})}_{m_{1}}\cdots\tilde{h}^{(a_{k})}_{m_{k}}\otimes e^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}}\left(\tilde{h}^{(a_{1})}_{m_{1}}\cdots\tilde{h}^{(a_{k})}_{m_{k}}\otimes e^{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varnothing\not=\{b_{1},\dots,b_{l}\}\\
\hskip 16.38895pt\subseteq\{a_{1},\dots,a_{k}\}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{l}\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{b_{1}}-\cdots-\epsilon_{b_{l}}}\left(\tilde{h}^{(a_{1})}_{m_{1}-u_{1}}\cdots\tilde{h}^{(a_{k})}_{m_{k}-u_{k}}\otimes e^{\alpha}\right),

or rather

(C_{\alpha}-\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}})\left(\tilde{h}^{(a_{1})}_{m_{1}}\cdots\tilde{h}^{(a_{k})}_{m_{k}}\otimes e^{\alpha}\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varnothing\not=\{b_{1},\dots,b_{l}\}\\
\hskip 16.38895pt\subseteq\{a_{1},\dots,a_{k}\}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{l}\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{b_{1}}-\cdots-\epsilon_{b_{l}}}\left(\tilde{h}^{(a_{1})}_{m_{1}-u_{1}}\cdots\tilde{h}^{(a_{k})}_{m_{k}-u_{k}}\otimes e^{\alpha}\right),

where in the last sum,
u_{i}=\chi(a_{i}\in\{b_{1},\dots,b_{l}\})
indicates if
a_{i}
is in the set of
b_{j}
’s.

By Equation (
4.8
), we can write

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{b_{1}}-\cdots-\epsilon_{b_{k}}}

\displaystyle=\left[\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{b_{1}}-\cdots-\epsilon_{b_{k-1}}},\tilde{p}_{1}^{(b_{k})}\right]

\displaystyle=\left[\left[\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{b_{1}}-\cdots-\epsilon_{b_{k-2}}},\tilde{p}_{1}^{(b_{k-1})}\right],\tilde{p}_{1}^{(b_{k})}\right]

(4.22)

\displaystyle=\left[\ldots\left[\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{b_{1}}},\tilde{p}_{1}^{(b_{2})}\right],\dots,\tilde{p}_{1}^{(b_{k})}\right],

and by induction on degree, we can now assume that there is a noncommutative polynomial
P_{m_{1},\dots,m_{k}}
for which

(C_{\alpha}-\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}})\left(\tilde{h}^{(a_{1})}_{m_{1}}\cdots\tilde{h}^{(a_{k})}_{m_{k}}\otimes e^{\alpha}\right)=P_{m_{1},\dots,m_{k}}\left(\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{i}},\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}\right)(1\otimes e^{\alpha}).

This implies that for the wreath Macdonald basis, for
\lambda
with
\mathrm{core}(\lambda)=\alpha
,

(C_{\alpha}-\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}})\left(H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\alpha}\right)=P_{H_{\lambda}}\left(\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{i}},\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}\right)(1\otimes e^{\alpha})

for some noncommutative polynomial
P_{H_{\lambda}}
.
But

(C_{\alpha}-\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}})\left(H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\alpha}\right)=\left(B_{\lambda}^{(0)}-B_{\alpha}^{(0)}\right)H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\alpha},

which, outside of the trivial case of
H_{\lambda}=H_{\alpha}=1
, is a nonzero scalar multiple.
The result then follows for
H_{\lambda}
by dividing
P_{H_{\lambda}}
by this scalar.
Since
\{H_{\lambda}\,|\,\mathrm{core}(\lambda)=\alpha\}
is a basis of
\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}
, the result holds for any
f
.
∎

Corollary 4.7
.

For any
f\in\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}
of degree
k
, we can write

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}A_{i}+\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{i}}B_{i}=\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}A^{*}_{i}+\mathbb{D}^{*}_{-\epsilon_{i}}B_{i}^{*}

for some
A_{i},B_{i},A^{*}_{i},B^{*}_{i}\in\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}
of degree
k-1
.

Proof.

Note that
\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}
,
\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{i}}
, and
\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{i}}^{*}
increase degree by
1
.
∎

Corollary 4.8
.

Every element
f\otimes e^{\alpha}\in\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}\otimes\mathbb{C}[Q]
may be written as

\displaystyle f\otimes e^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=P\left(\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}},\tilde{p}_{1}^{(0)},\ldots,\tilde{p}_{1}^{(r-1)}\right)(1\otimes e^{\alpha})

\displaystyle=P^{*}\left(\mathbb{D}^{*}_{\vec{0}},\tilde{p}_{1}^{(0)},\ldots,\tilde{p}_{1}^{(r-1)}\right)(1\otimes e^{\alpha})

for some noncommutative polynomial
P
in the operators
\{\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}},\tilde{p}_{1}^{(0)},\ldots,\tilde{p}_{1}^{(r-1)}\}
and some noncommutative polynomial
P^{*}
in
\{\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}}^{*},\tilde{p}_{1}^{(0)},\ldots,\tilde{p}_{1}^{(r-1)}\}
.

Proof.

Combine Theorem
4.6
with (
4.8
) and (
4.10
), utilizing

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{i}}=\left[\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}},\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}\right]

\displaystyle\text{ and }

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}^{*}_{-\epsilon_{i}}=-q^{-1}t^{-1}\left[\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}}^{*},\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}\right].

∎

4.2.
Delta functions

We now introduce the following “delta functions”:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle\coloneq\Omega\left[\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}\left(\frac{D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(t-1)}\right)^{(i)}\right]

\displaystyle=\Omega\left[\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}\left(\left(\frac{1}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(i)}-B_{\lambda}^{(i)}\right)\right]

It is an element in the completion of
\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}
by degree.

Proposition 4.9
.

For any
r
-core
\alpha
,
\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}
can be written as

\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\\
\mathrm{core}(\mu)=\alpha\end{subarray}}\frac{H_{\mu}^{\dagger}[X^{\bullet}]H_{\mu}[\iota D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}]}{N_{\mu}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\\
\mathrm{core}(\mu)=\alpha\end{subarray}}\frac{H_{\mu}[X^{\bullet}]H_{\mu}^{\dagger}[\iota D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}]}{N_{\mu}}.

Proof.

We obtain
\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}[X^{\bullet}]
from
\Omega_{q,t}[X^{-\bullet}Y^{\bullet}]
by (1) applying
\iota
to the
Y^{\bullet}
-variables and then (2) specializing them to
D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}
:

\displaystyle\Omega_{q,t}[X^{-\bullet}Y^{\bullet}]

\displaystyle=\Omega\left[X^{(0)}\left(\frac{Y^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right)+\cdots+X^{(r-1)}\left(\frac{Y^{(1)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\mapsto}\Omega\left[X^{(0)}\left(\frac{Y^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma)(t\sigma^{-1}-1)}\right)+\cdots+X^{(r-1)}\left(\frac{Y^{(r-1)}}{(1-q\sigma)(t\sigma^{-1}-1)}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(2)}{\mapsto}\Omega\left[X^{(0)}\left(\frac{D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(t-1)}\right)^{(0)}+\cdots+X^{(r-1)}\left(\frac{D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(t-1)}\right)^{(r-1)}\right].\

The equations follow from Proposition
2.14
.
∎

Corollary 4.10
.

\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}
is a delta function for the
\iota
-twisted evaluation at
D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}
:

\langle f,\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\rangle_{q,t}^{\prime}=f[\iota D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}].

Corollary 4.11
.

The set
\{\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\}
is linearly independent in the completion of
\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}
.

4.2.1.
Delta on delta

To investigate how a Delta operator
\Delta^{\dagger}[f]
acts on
\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}
, we will need to instead consider its action on
\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}
.

Lemma 4.12
.

Recall the elements
\hat{e}_{n}^{(p)}
and
\hat{h}_{n}^{(p)}
from
2.3.2
.

(1)

We have

\Delta^{\dagger}\left[\hat{e}_{n}^{(p)}[-\iota X^{\bullet}]\right]\left(\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\right)=\sum_{\mu}c_{\mu\lambda}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}

where the
\mu
that appear are such that:

•

\mu\supset\lambda
;

•

\mu\backslash\lambda
contains
n
boxes of each color;

•

no color
p
and color
(p+1)
box in
\mu\backslash\lambda
are horizontally adjacent.

(2)

Similarly, we have

\Delta^{\dagger}\left[\hat{h}_{n}^{(p)}[-\iota X^{\bullet}]\right]\left(\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\right)=\sum_{\mu}d_{\mu\lambda}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}

where the
\mu
that appear are such that:

•

\mu\supset\lambda
;

•

\mu\backslash\lambda
contains
n
boxes of each color;

•

no color
p
and color
(p+1)
box in
\mu\backslash\lambda
are vertically adjacent.

Proof.

First consider the action of the translation components of both Delta operators (
3.16
) and (
3.17
) for a variable
z_{i,a}
:

\displaystyle\TT\left[\left(-tX^{(i-1)}+(1+qt)X^{(i)}-qX^{(i+1)}\right)z_{i,a}^{-1}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\Omega\left[\left(-t\left(\frac{D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(t-1)}\right)^{(i-1)}+(1+qt)\left(\frac{D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(t-1)}\right)^{(i)}-q\left(\frac{D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(t-1)}\right)^{(i+1)}\right)z_{i,a}^{-1}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\Omega\left[\left(\frac{(1-q)(1-t)D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(t-1)}\right)^{(i)}z_{i,a}^{-1}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\Omega\left[-D_{\lambda}^{(i)}z_{i,a}^{-1}\right]\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}

(4.23)

\displaystyle=\frac{\displaystyle\prod_{\square\in R_{i}(\lambda)}\left(1-qt\chi_{\square}/z_{i,a}\right)}{\displaystyle\prod_{\square\in A_{i}(\lambda)}\left(1-\chi_{\square}/z_{i,a}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}.

For the last equality, we have used (
2.4
).
Next, we look at
z_{i,a}^{-H_{i,0}}e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}
.
As in the proof of Lemma
3.11
, we can ignore the powers of
\mathfrak{d}
because we have an equal number of such operators per color.
Setting
\mathrm{core}(\lambda)=(c_{0},\ldots,c_{r-1})
, equation (
2.1
) gives us

z_{i,a}^{-H_{i,0}}e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}=z_{i,a}^{\#R_{i}(\lambda)-\#A_{i}(\lambda)+\delta_{i,0}}e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}

We will use this balance out the binomials in (
4.23
).
Our final piece of preparation is to absorb the
\Omega
-components of the operators into
\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}
.
In total, after balancing all the remaining binomials, we have

(4.24)

\displaystyle\Delta^{\dagger}\left[\hat{e}_{n}^{(p)}[-\iota X^{\bullet}]\right]\left(\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{t^{n}(1-qt)^{nr}}{\prod_{k=1}^{n}(1-q^{k}t^{k})}\left\{\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq n}\left[\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\frac{\left(z_{p,b}-z_{p,a}\right)\left(z_{p,b}-qtz_{p,a}\right)}{\left(z_{p,b}-t^{-1}z_{p+1,a}\right)\left(z_{p,b}-tz_{p-1,a}\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{p\}}\frac{\left(z_{i,b}-z_{i,a}\right)\left(z_{i,b}-qtz_{i,a}\right)}{\left(z_{i,b}-qz_{i+1,a}\right)\left(z_{i,b}-tz_{i-1,a}\right)}\left]\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.

\displaystyle\times\prod_{a=1}^{n}\left[\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{p\}}\left(\frac{z_{i+1,a}}{z_{i+1,a}-tz_{i,a}}\right)\left(\frac{z_{i,a}}{z_{i,a}-qz_{i+1,a}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{z_{0,a}}{z_{p+1,a}}\right)\left(\frac{z_{p+1,a}}{z_{p+1,a}-tz_{p,a}}\right)\left]\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\prod_{a=1}^{n}z_{0,a}\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\displaystyle\prod_{\square\in R_{i}(\lambda)}\left(z_{i,a}-qt\chi_{\square}\right)}{\displaystyle\prod_{\square\in A_{i}(\lambda)}\left(z_{i,a}-\chi_{\square}\right)}

\displaystyle\left.\quad\times\Omega\left[\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}\left(\left(\frac{1}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(i)}-B_{\lambda}^{(i)}-\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}\right)\right]\right\}_{0}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)},

and

(4.25)

\displaystyle\Delta^{\dagger}\left[\hat{h}_{n}^{(p)}[-\iota X^{\bullet}]\right]\left(\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{n}\left(1-qt\right)^{nr}}{\prod_{k=1}^{n}\left(1-q^{k}t^{k}\right)}\left\{\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq n}\left[\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\frac{\left(z_{p,b}-z_{p,a}\right)\left(z_{p,b}-qtz_{p,a}\right)}{\left(z_{p,b}-q^{-1}z_{p-1,a}\right)\left(z_{p,b}-qz_{p+1,a}\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{p\}}\frac{\left(z_{i,b}-z_{i,a}\right)\left(z_{i,b}-qtz_{i,a}\right)}{\left(z_{i,b}-qz_{i+1,a}\right)\left(z_{i,b}-tz_{i-1,a}\right)}\left]\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.

\displaystyle\times\prod_{a=1}^{n}\left[\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{p\}}\left(\frac{z_{i-1,a}}{z_{i-1,a}-qz_{i,a}}\right)\left(\frac{z_{i,a}}{z_{i,a}-tz_{i-1,a}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\left(\frac{z_{0,a}}{z_{p,a}}\right)\left(\frac{z_{p-1,a}}{z_{p-1,a}-qz_{p,a}}\right)\left]\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\prod_{a=1}^{n}z_{0,a}\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\displaystyle\prod_{\square\in R_{i}(\lambda)}\left(z_{i,a}-qt\chi_{\square}\right)}{\displaystyle\prod_{\square\in A_{i}(\lambda)}\left(z_{i,a}-\chi_{\square}\right)}

\displaystyle\left.\quad\times\Omega\left[\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}\left(\left(\frac{1}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(i)}-B_{\lambda}^{(i)}-\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}\right)\right]\right\}_{0}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}.

In both formulas, the rational functions are Laurent series expanded assuming

\displaystyle|z_{i,a}|

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle|q|,|t|

\displaystyle<1

For technical reasons, we will introduce an auxilliary variable
\mathfrak{p}
.
The constant term (
4.24
) is equal to

(4.26)

\displaystyle\frac{t^{n}(1-qt)^{nr}}{\prod_{k=1}^{n}(1-q^{k}t^{k})}\left\{\prod_{1\leq a<b\leq n}\left[\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\frac{\left(z_{p,b}-z_{p,a}\right)\left(z_{p,b}-qtz_{p,a}\right)}{\left(z_{p,b}-\mathfrak{p}^{-1}z_{p+1,a}\right)\left(z_{p,b}-\mathfrak{p}z_{p-1,a}\right)}\right.

(4.27)

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}\backslash\{p\}}\frac{\left(z_{i,b}-z_{i,a}\right)\left(z_{i,b}-qtz_{i,a}\right)}{\left(z_{i,b}-qz_{i+1,a}\right)\left(z_{i,b}-\mathfrak{p}z_{i-1,a}\right)}\left]\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.

(4.28)

\displaystyle\quad\times\prod_{a=1}^{n}\left[\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{p\}}\left(\frac{z_{i+1,a}}{z_{i+1,a}-\mathfrak{p}z_{i,a}}\right)\left(\frac{z_{i,a}}{z_{i,a}-qz_{i+1,a}}\right)

(4.29)

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\frac{z_{0,a}}{z_{p+1,a}}\right)\left(\frac{z_{p+1,a}}{z_{p+1,a}-\mathfrak{p}z_{p,a}}\right)\left]\rule{0.0pt}{22.0pt}\right.\prod_{a=1}^{n}z_{0,a}\prod_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\displaystyle\prod_{\square\in R_{i}(\lambda)}\left(z_{i,a}-qt\chi_{\square}\right)}{\displaystyle\prod_{\square\in A_{i}(\lambda)}\left(z_{i,a}-\chi_{\square}\right)}

(4.30)

\displaystyle\left.\quad\times\Omega\left[\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}\left(\left(\frac{1}{(1-q)(1-t)}\right)^{(i)}-B_{\lambda}^{(i)}-\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{i,a}\right)\right]\right\}_{0}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\bigg|_{\mathfrak{p}\mapsto t}

where the denominators are expanded assuming

\displaystyle|z_{i,a}|

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle|q|,|t|

\displaystyle<1,

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{p}|<|q^{a}t^{b}|\hbox{ for all }(a,b)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}.

We will only present the proof of part (1) since part (2) is analyzed in a similar way; but we also note that after swapping
q\leftrightarrow t
in (
4.25
) and negating the color of variables, we obtain the formula for
\Delta^{\dagger}[\hat{e}_{n}^{(-p-1)}[-\iota X^{\bullet}]]
.

The strategy
: We apply Lemma
4.1
, starting with
z_{p,1}
and proceeding
downward
in cyclic order to
z_{p-1,1}
, then
z_{p-2,1}
, and so on. At each step, we will be evaluating
z_{i,1}
at the character of a box; and in (
4.30
) this corresponds to adding a character to
B_{\lambda}^{(i)}
. We will then proceed similarly with the remaining
z_{i,a}
’s, as we will describe.
In the end, we will arrive at some
B_{\mu}^{\bullet}
, and the goal is to show that the boxes which are added produce a partition
\mu
which satisfies the conditions from the lemma.

Now, for
z_{p,1}
, we can always find a power of
z_{p,1}
in the numerator of (
4.28
); and
there are only two kinds of poles where
z_{p,1}
is expanded in negative powers:

(
\chi
)

\chi
-poles
: for some
\square\in A_{p}(\lambda)
, the pole
(z_{p,1}-\chi_{\square})
in (
4.29
);

(
t
)

t
-pole
: the pole
(z_{p,1}-\mathfrak{p}z_{p-1,1})
in (
4.28
).

Thus, we have

\frac{z_{p,1}F(z_{p,1})}{(z_{p,1}-\mathfrak{p}z_{p-1,1})\displaystyle\prod_{\square\in A_{p}(\lambda)}(z_{p,1}-\chi_{\square})},

where
F(z_{p,1})
is a power series in
z_{p,1}
.
Applying Lemma
4.1
, we split into the cases where
z_{p,1}
is evaluated at a
\chi
-pole or a
t
-pole.
In the first case, in (
4.30
),
B_{\lambda}^{(p)}
is appended with an addable box and thus we still have a partition.

As we progress downward in cyclic order along the color index to
z_{i,1}
with
i\not=p,p+1
,
we note that there is a
z_{i-1,1}^{2}
in the numerator of (
4.28
); and
the poles where
z_{i,1}
is expanded negatively include
t
- and
\chi
-poles.
But we also now have new kinds of poles we call a

(
q
)

q
-pole
: the pole
(z_{i,1}-qz_{i+1,1})
in (
4.28
).

If the previous variable
z_{i+1,1}
was evaluated at the
t
-pole
(z_{i+1,1}-\mathfrak{p}z_{i,1})
, then we have

(4.31)

\frac{z_{i,1}}{z_{i,1}-qz_{i+1}}\mapsto\frac{z_{i,1}}{z_{i,1}-q\mathfrak{p}z_{i,1}}=(1-q\mathfrak{p})^{-1}

in (
4.28
), and so
z_{i,1}
has no
q
-pole.
Also note that if
z_{i+1,1}
was evaluated at the
t
-pole, then one must also consider poles in (
4.29
) of the form

\mathfrak{p}^{k}z_{i,1}-\chi_{\square}

for some
0<k<r
and
\square\in A_{i+k}(\lambda)
.
However, because
|\mathfrak{p}|<|q^{a}t^{b}|
, these are not expanded in negative powers of
z_{i-1}
.
Thus, the evaluations occur in:

•

horizontal chains
initiated at a
\chi
-pole evaluation followed by consecutive
q
-pole evaluations;

•

vertical chains
of consecutive
t
-pole evaluations that terminate at a
\chi
-pole evaluation;

•

a chain of consecutive
t
-pole evaluations leading up to
z_{p+1,1}
.

The first two append characters to
B_{\lambda}^{(i)}
in (
4.30
) upon the evaluation
\mathfrak{p}\mapsto t
.
Notice that here,
z_{i,1}
is evaluated at the character of a box of color
i
.
The only way these additions might fail to yield a partition is if for some
i
,
z_{i,1}=qt\chi_{\square}
for some
\square\in R_{i}(\lambda)
.
This is prevented by the zero
(z_{i,1}-qt\chi_{\square})
in (
4.29
), which can only be canceled away by the pole
(z_{i,1}-qz_{i+1})
if
z_{i+1,1}
is evaluated at
t\chi_{\square}
(i.e. the box additions successfully complete the corner).

At
z_{p+1,1}
, two new considerations arise.
First,
z_{p+1,1}
only has a single power in the numerator from (
4.28
) and (
4.29
).
As in (
4.31
), if
z_{p+2,1}
was evaluated at the
t
-pole
(z_{p+2,1}-\mathfrak{p}z_{p+1,1})
, that power of
z_{p+1,1}
may be canceled away.
However, in this case, the initiating variable of this chain of consecutive
t
-poles will have a leftover power remaining that is evaluated at
\mathfrak{p}^{k}z_{p+1,}
for some
k
.
Thus, we will always have a positive power of
z_{p+1,1}
to implement Lemma
4.1
.
Second, there is a new kind of pole expanded in negative powers of
z_{p+1,1}
:

(
t^{\uparrow}
)

t^{\uparrow}
-pole
: for
b>1
,
(z_{p,b}-\mathfrak{p}^{-1}z_{p+1,1})
in (
4.26
).

If we evaluate
z_{p+1,1}
at such a pole, then we continue with
z_{p,b}
.
It only has the following poles expanded in negative powers of
z_{p,b}
:

(
\chi
)

\chi
-poles
: for some
\square\in A_{p}(\lambda)
, the pole
(z_{p,b}-\chi_{\square})
in (
4.29
);

(
t
)

t
-poles
: for
a\leq b
,
(z_{p,b}-\mathfrak{p}z_{p-1,a})
in (
4.26
).

Only evaluation at the latter has the possibility of not evaluating at some
\chi_{\square}
; thus, this chain of
t
-pole evaluations continues.
The plan is to follow this chain of
t
-poles until it finally evaluates at a character.
Suppose that along this chain, we have evaluated
z_{i+1,c_{1}}
at the pole
z_{i+1,c_{1}}-\mathfrak{p}z_{i,c_{2}}
for
c_{2}<c_{1}
, and consider now
z_{i,c_{2}}
.
Like for
z_{p,b}
, we have
\chi
- and
t
-poles, but we may also need to consider

(
q
)

q
-poles
: for
c_{3}\leq c_{2}
,
(z_{i,c_{2}}-qz_{i+1,c_{3}})
.

However, since
c_{1}\geq c_{3}
, either
c_{1}=c_{3}
and this pole has been canceled as in (
4.31
) or
c_{1}<c_{3}
, in which case the result vanishes when
\mathfrak{p}\mapsto t
due to the factor
(z_{i+1,c_{3}}-qtz_{i+1,c_{1}})
from (
4.26
).
Finally, when
i=p+1
, there is also a
t^{\uparrow}
-pole that continues the chain.
When we finally evaluate at a character
\chi_{\square}
, the zeros
(z_{i,b}-z_{i,a})
prevent duplicate evaluations while
(z_{i,a}-qt\chi_{\square})
for
\square\in R_{i}(\lambda)
in (
4.29
) and
(z_{i,b}-qtz_{i,1})
in (
4.26
) will ensure that the box additions to
B_{\lambda}^{(i)}
in (
4.30
) result in a partition.
Note that the removable box at the top of this vertical chain comes from evaluating a
z_{i,1}
for some
i
.

After chasing down such a chain of
t
-poles resulting from evaluating
z_{p+1,1}
at a
t^{\uparrow}
-pole, we continue onto
z_{p,2}
.
We may have variables that have already been evaluated in the vertical chain involving the
t^{\uparrow}
-pole, in which case we move down in cyclic order along the color index until we hit
p+1
, after which we move up in the second index.
The analysis is as before: we continue step by step and chase down any chain resulting from a
t^{\uparrow}
-pole.
Zeroes of the form
(z_{i,b}-z_{i,a})
in (
4.26
) prevent duplicate evaluations, while those of the form
(z_{i,a}-qt\chi_{\square})
for
\square\in R_{i}(\lambda)
in (
4.29
) and
(z_{i,b}-qtz_{i,a})
in (
4.26
) keep the characters in (
4.30
) to those of a partition.

At the end of this process, the box additions in (
4.30
) result in
B_{\mu}^{\bullet}
for some partition
\mu
, and no color
p
and
(p+1)
boxes in
\mu\backslash\lambda
are horizontally adjacent due to the lack of
q
-poles for
z_{p+1,a}
.
∎

Corollary 4.13
.

The span of
\{\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\}
is closed under the action of
\Delta^{\dagger}\left[f\right]
for any
f\in\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}
.

4.2.2.
The map
\mathsf{V}

By Corollary
4.13
, we can define the following important map.

Definition 4.14
.

The linear map

\mathsf{V}:\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}\otimes\mathbb{C}[Q]\rightarrow\mathrm{span}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\right\}

is defined by setting two conditions:

•

\mathsf{V}(fg\otimes e^{\alpha})=\Delta^{\dagger}\left[f[-\iota X^{\bullet}]\right]\left(\mathsf{V}\left(g\otimes e^{\alpha}\right)\right)
,

•

\mathsf{V}(1\otimes e^{\alpha})=\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\otimes e^{\alpha}
.

Theorem 4.15
.

We have

\mathsf{V}(H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)})=H_{\lambda}[\iota D_{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}^{\bullet}]\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}

Proof.

This theorem follows from the combinatorial results of Section 5 from
[
Wen19
]
.
Specifically, we have
\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}
acting on a vector space with a basis
\{\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\}
indexed by partitions, where
f\in\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}
acts via the operator
\Delta^{\dagger}\left[f[-\iota X^{\bullet}]\right]
.
For a partition
\lambda
, we consider the expansions of

(4.32)

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}\left(\hat{e}_{\mathrm{quot}(\lambda)}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu}a_{\lambda\mu}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}~\text{ and }

(4.33)

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}\left(\hat{h}_{\mathrm{quot}(\lambda)}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mu}b_{\lambda\mu}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}.

The actions of
\hat{e}_{n}^{(p)}
and
\hat{h}_{n}^{(p)}
add boxes to the partition index as prescribed in Lemma
4.12
.
Thus, for any
\mu
appearing on the right-hand-side of (
4.32
), each ordering
\mathcal{O}_{c}
of the columns of
\mathrm{quot}(\lambda)
endows
\mu
with a certain kind of tableau, wherein each labeling records the boxes added from the action of a
\hat{e}_{n}^{(p)}
.
These are the
row-strict
r
-tableaux
of content
(\lambda,\mathcal{O}_{c})
from
loc. cit.

Similarly, the
\mu
in the right-hand-side of (
4.33
) possess a certain kind of tableau for each ordering
\mathcal{O}_{r}
of the rows in
\mathrm{quot}(\lambda)
; these are called
column-strict
r
-tableaux
of content
(\lambda,\mathcal{O}_{r})
.

Additionally, these
\mu
must satisfy a nested condition.
On the left-hand-side of (
4.32
), we can forget some columns of
\mathrm{quot}(\lambda)
to obtain
\mathrm{quot}(\lambda^{\prime})
for some smaller partition where
\mathrm{core}(\lambda)=\mathrm{core}(\lambda^{\prime})
; we take the smaller product
\hat{e}_{\mathrm{quot}(\lambda^{\prime})}
.
Let
\mathcal{O}_{c}
be an ordering of the columns of
\mathrm{quot}(\lambda)
where the removed columns are at the end.
In terms of
\mu
, the column removal corresponds to taking a row-strict column tableau on
\mu
of content
(\lambda,\mathcal{O}_{c})
and cutting off the boxes corresponding to the removed columns, yielding the smaller partition
\mu^{\prime}
.
It should then be possible to produce such a
\mu^{\prime}
that has nonzero coefficient in the expansion for
\hat{e}_{\mathrm{quot}(\lambda^{\prime})}
.

All-in-all, the existence of the row-strict
r
-tableaux and this nesting condition is summarized in
loc. cit.
in the definition of
strongly row-strict
\lambda
-tabloidizable
.
The
\mu
appearing on the right-hand-side of (
4.32
) must be strongly row-strict
\lambda
-tabloidizable, and by Lemma 5.6 of
loc. cit.
, this implies
\mu\leq_{r}\lambda
.
Likewise, the
\mu
appearing on the right-hand-side of (
4.33
) are
strongly column-strict
\lambda
-tabloidizable
, which by the same lemma implies
\mu\geq_{r}\lambda
.
These together imply that given
\lambda
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}\left(\mathrm{span}\left\{\hat{e}_{\mathrm{quot}(\mu)}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\,\middle|\,\mu\leq_{r}\lambda\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\subset\mathrm{span}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\,\middle|\,\mu\leq_{r}\lambda\right\}~\text{ and }

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}\left(\mathrm{span}\left\{\hat{h}_{\mathrm{quot}(\mu)}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\,\middle|\,\mu\geq_{r}\lambda\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\subset\mathrm{span}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\,\middle|\,\mu\geq_{r}\lambda\right\}.

By Proposition
2.9
, it follows that
\mathsf{V}(H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)})
is a scalar multiple of
\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}
.

To compute that scalar, first note that

(4.34)

\mathsf{V}\left(H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\right)=\Delta^{\dagger}\left[H_{\lambda}[-\iota X^{\bullet}]\right]\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\right).

It suffices to compare the constant term of the right-hand-side of (
4.34
) with that of
\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}
.
To that end, we use Proposition
4.9
to expand
\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}
in the basis
\{H_{\mu}^{\dagger}\,|\,\mathrm{core}(\mu)=w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)\}
:

\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\\
\mathrm{core}(\mu)=w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)\end{subarray}}\frac{H_{\mu}^{\dagger}[X^{\bullet}]H_{\mu}[\iota D_{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}^{\bullet}]}{N_{\mu}}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}.

By (
3.15
), the right-hand-side of (
4.34
) is:

(4.35)

\Delta^{\dagger}\left[H_{\lambda}[-\iota X^{\bullet}]\right]\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\\
\mathrm{core}(\mu)=w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)\end{subarray}}\frac{H_{\mu}^{\dagger}[X^{\bullet}]H_{\lambda}[\iota D_{\mu}^{\bullet}]H_{\mu}[\iota D_{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}^{\bullet}]}{N_{\mu}}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}.

The constant term is the summand where
\mu=w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)
, in which case the right hand side of (
4.35
) gives us
H_{\lambda}[\iota D_{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}^{\bullet}]\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}
.
On the other hand, from Proposition
4.9
, the constant term of
\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}
is
1\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}
.
∎

Our main task will be to give an explicit formula for
\mathsf{V}
.
Note that despite lacking such a formula, we can already deduce reciprocity:

Corollary 4.16
.

For partitions
\lambda
and
\mu
with
\mathrm{core}(\mu)=w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)
, the equality holds:

\frac{H_{\mu}\left[\iota D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}\right]}{H_{\mu}\left[\iota D^{\bullet}_{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\mu)}\right]}=\frac{H_{\lambda}\left[\iota D^{\bullet}_{\mu}\right]}{H_{\lambda}\left[\iota D^{\bullet}_{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\right]}.

Proof.

Compare the coefficient of
H_{\mu}^{\dagger}[X^{\bullet}]
in (
4.35
) with that of
H_{\lambda}[\iota D_{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}^{\bullet}]\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}
given by using Proposition
4.9
.
∎

4.2.3.
The main properties of
\mathsf{V}

We reiterate here the main properties of
\mathsf{V}
.
In the next section, we will give an explicit expression for
\mathsf{V}
, as in Tesler’s Identity, showing, in the end, that these identities hold for the explicit version.

Proposition 4.17
.

For any
f\in\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}
, let
\underline{f}
denote the operation of multiplication by
f
. We have

•

\mathsf{V}\underline{f}=\Delta^{\dagger}\left[f[-\iota X^{\bullet}]\right]\mathsf{V}
,

•

f^{\perp}\mathsf{V}=\mathsf{V}\Delta\left[f\left[\frac{X^{\bullet}}{(1-q\sigma)(1-t\sigma^{-1})}\right]\right]
,

•

\mathsf{V}(1\otimes e^{\alpha})=\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\otimes e^{\alpha},

•

\mathsf{V}\left(H_{\lambda}[X^{\bullet}]\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\right)=H_{\lambda}[\iota D_{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}^{\bullet}]\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}.

Proof.

The first and third properties are from the definition of
\mathsf{V}
.
The last property follows from Theorem
4.15
.
The second condition follows from these. We will show that

h_{n}^{\perp}[X^{(j)}]\mathsf{V}=\mathsf{V}\Delta\left[h_{n}\left[\frac{X^{(j)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(1-t\sigma)}\right]\right]

by applying
\TT[vX^{(j)}]=\sum_{n\geq 0}v^{n}h_{n}^{\perp}[X^{(j)}]
to
\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}
:

\displaystyle\TT[vX^{(j)}]\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}

\displaystyle=\TT[vX^{(j)}]\Omega\left[\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}\left(\frac{D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(t-1)}\right)^{(i)}\right]\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}

\displaystyle=\Omega\left[\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}\left(X^{(i)}+\delta_{i,j}v\right)\left(\frac{D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(t-1)}\right)^{(i)}\right]\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}

\displaystyle=\Omega\left[v\left(\frac{D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(t-1)}\right)^{(j)}\right]\Omega\left[\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/r\mathbb{Z}}X^{(i)}\left(\frac{D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(t-1)}\right)^{(i)}\right]\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{V}\sum_{n\geq 0}v^{n}h_{n}\left[\left(\frac{D_{\lambda}^{\bullet}}{(1-q)(t-1)}\right)^{(j)}\right]H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{V}\sum_{n\geq 0}v^{n}\Delta\left[h_{n}\left[\frac{X^{(j)}}{(1-q\sigma)(1-t\sigma^{-1})}\right]\right]H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}.

Comparing the coefficient of
v^{n}
on both sides gives the desired property.
∎

4.3.
Proving the identity

Here, we state and prove the main result of the paper:

Theorem 4.18
.

The operator
\mathsf{V}
from Definition
4.14
is given by

\mathsf{V}=\nabla\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right]\TT\left[X^{(0)}\right]\nabla

Thus, when combined with Theorem
4.15
, we obtain the Tesler formula:

\mathbb{E}_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}=\nabla\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right]\TT\left[X^{(0)}\right]\nabla\left(\frac{H_{\lambda}}{H_{\lambda}[\iota D_{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}^{\bullet}]}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\right)

4.3.1.
The eigenvalue of
\nabla

Before proceeding to the proof of Theorem
4.18
, we make a slight interlude and mention a corollary of the theorem.

Corollary 4.19
.

Recall the eigenvalue of
\nabla
from (
2.6
).
The following equalities hold:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}H_{\lambda}=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\square\in\lambda\backslash\mathrm{core}(\lambda)\\
\bar{c}_{\square}=0)\end{subarray}}(-\chi_{\square})=H_{\lambda}[\iota D_{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}^{\bullet}].

Proof.

By Theorem
4.18
and the defintion of
\mathsf{V}
, we have

(4.36)

\mathbb{E}_{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\otimes e^{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}=\nabla\left(\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right]\otimes e^{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\right)

Consider the right-hand-side of (
4.36
).
First, note that by Proposition
2.14
,

\displaystyle\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right]

\displaystyle=\Omega_{q,t}\left[Y^{-\bullet}X^{\bullet}\right]\bigg|_{Y^{(i)}\mapsto\delta_{i,0}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\\
\mathrm{core}(\mu)=\mathrm{core}(\lambda)\end{subarray}}\frac{H_{\mu}[1]H_{\mu}^{\dagger}[X^{\bullet}]}{N_{\mu}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\\
\mathrm{core}(\mu)=\mathrm{core}(\lambda)\end{subarray}}\frac{H_{\mu}^{\dagger}[X^{\bullet}]}{N_{\mu}},

because we have normalized
H_{\mu}
such that
H_{\mu}[1]=1
.
Therefore, by Corollary
3.32
, the right-hand-side of (
4.36
) is

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\\
\mathrm{core}(\mu)=\mathrm{core}(\lambda)\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\frac{\nabla_{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}H_{\mu}^{\dagger}[X^{\bullet}]}{N_{\mu}}\otimes e^{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}

(4.37)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\\
\mathrm{core}(\mu)=\mathrm{core}(\lambda)\end{subarray}}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\square\in\mu\backslash\mathrm{core}(\mu)\\
\bar{c}_{\square}=0)\end{subarray}}(-\chi_{\square})\right)\frac{H_{\mu}^{\dagger}[X^{\bullet}]}{N_{\mu}}\otimes e^{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}.

On the other hand, by Proposition
4.9
, the left-hand-side of (
4.36
) is

(4.38)

\mathbb{E}_{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\otimes e^{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\\
\mathrm{core}(\mu)=\mathrm{core}(\lambda)\end{subarray}}\frac{H_{\mu}^{\dagger}[X^{\bullet}]H_{\mu}[\iota D_{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}^{\bullet}]}{N_{\mu}}\otimes e^{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}.

The result follows from comparing the coefficients of
H_{\lambda}^{\dagger}[X^{\bullet}]
in (
4.37
) and (
4.38
).
∎

4.3.2.
Reduction of the conditions

Using Corollary
4.8
, we can simplify the defining conditions for
\mathsf{V}
even further:

Proposition 4.20
.

\mathsf{V}
is determined by the following three conditions:

•

Commuting with
\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}}
:

(4.39)

\mathsf{V}\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}}=p_{1}^{\perp}[X^{(0)}]\mathsf{V}.

•

Commuting with
\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}
:

(4.40)

\mathsf{V}\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}=-\mathbb{D}_{\epsilon_{0}-\epsilon_{i}}\mathsf{V}.

•

Base case:

\mathsf{V}(1\otimes e^{\alpha})=\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\otimes e^{\alpha}.

We first prove the first two commutation relations and prove the
base case
in the subsequent sections.

Proof.

Applying Corollary
4.8
, we can reduce the defining condition

\mathsf{V}(f\otimes e^{\alpha})=\Delta^{\dagger}\left[f[-\iota X^{\bullet}]\right]\left(\mathsf{V}\left(1\otimes e^{\alpha}\right)\right)

to commutation relations with
\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}}
and
\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}
.
These are given by (
4.39
) and (
4.40
).
To see that, we apply Proposition
4.17
, noting that

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}}

\displaystyle=\Delta\left[p_{1}\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma)(1-t\sigma^{-1})}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{\epsilon_{0}-\epsilon_{i}}

\displaystyle=\Delta^{\dagger}\left[\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}[\iota X^{\bullet}]\right].\qed

4.3.3.
Commutation relations

To prove the
base case
, we must first show

Proposition 4.21
.

The following hold:

(4.41)

\displaystyle\nabla\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right]\TT\left[X^{(0)}\right]\nabla\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}}

\displaystyle=p_{1}^{\perp}[X^{(0)}]\nabla\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right]\TT\left[X^{(0)}\right]\nabla,

(4.42)

\displaystyle\nabla\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right]\TT\left[X^{(0)}\right]\nabla\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{D}_{\epsilon_{0}-\epsilon_{i}}\nabla\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right]\TT\left[X^{(0)}\right]\nabla.

Both come from applying the relations from
4.1.4
.
Let us begin with (
4.41
).

\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}}
commutes with
\nabla
as they share the common eigenbasis
\{H_{\lambda}\otimes e^{\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\}
.
Applying (
4.4
) and (
4.5
) yields

\displaystyle\nabla\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right]\TT\left[X^{(0)}\right]\nabla\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}}

\displaystyle=\nabla\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right]\TT\left[X^{(0)}\right]\mathbb{D}_{\vec{0}}\nabla

\displaystyle=\left\{\left(\frac{1-z_{0}}{1-z_{0}^{-1}}\right)\nabla\mathbb{D}\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right]\TT\left[X^{(0)}\right]\nabla\right\}_{0}

\displaystyle=-\nabla\mathbb{D}_{\epsilon_{0}}\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right]\TT\left[X^{(0)}\right]\nabla.

Finally, we apply (
4.13
) to obtain the right-hand-side of (
4.41
).

For (
4.42
), we start with (
4.12
):

\displaystyle\nabla\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right]\TT\left[X^{(0)}\right]\nabla\tilde{p}_{1}^{(i)}

\displaystyle=\nabla\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right]\TT\left[X^{(0)}\right]\mathbb{D}_{-\epsilon_{i}}\nabla

\displaystyle=\left\{\left(\frac{1-z_{0}}{1-z_{0}^{-1}}\right)\nabla\mathbb{D}\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right]\TT\left[X^{(0)}\right]\nabla\right\}_{0}

\displaystyle=-\nabla\mathbb{D}_{\epsilon_{0}-\epsilon_{i}}\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right]\TT\left[X^{(0)}\right]\nabla,

where, as in the previous paragraph, we applied (
4.4
) and (
4.5
).
We can commute
\mathbb{D}_{\epsilon_{0}-\epsilon_{i}}
with
\nabla
because by Lemma
3.31
(1) and Corollary
3.32
, both operators share the eigenbasis
\{H_{\lambda}^{\dagger}\otimes e^{w_{0}\mathrm{core}(\lambda)}\}
.

4.3.4.
Proof of the base case

Here, we want to show

(4.43)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\otimes e^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\overset{?}{=}\nabla\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right]\TT\left[X^{(0)}\right]\nabla(1\otimes e^{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\nabla\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right](1\otimes e^{\alpha}).

Notice that because
H_{\lambda}[1]=1
, we have

(4.44)

\nabla\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right](1\otimes e^{\alpha})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mathrm{core}(\lambda)=w_{0}\alpha\end{subarray}}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\square\in\lambda\backslash\mathrm{core}(\lambda)\\
\bar{c}_{\square}=0\end{subarray}}(-\chi_{\square})\right)\frac{H_{\lambda}^{\dagger}}{N_{\lambda}}\otimes e^{\alpha}.

In order to prove (
4.43
), by Corollary
4.10
, it suffices to show that for any
f\in\Lambda_{q,t}^{\otimes r}
, we have

\left\langle f\otimes e^{w_{0}\alpha},\nabla\Omega\left[\frac{X^{(0)}}{(1-q\sigma^{-1})(t\sigma-1)}\right](1\otimes e^{\alpha})\right\rangle_{q,t}^{\prime}=f\left[\iota D_{\alpha}^{\bullet}\right].

By (
4.44
), this is obvious when
f=1
.
We will induct by degree.

If
\deg(f)=n
, by Corollary
4.7
, we can write
f
as
